# AUDIT — MASTER TOC AGAINST THE BOOK BODY, ALL 58 CHAPTERS

Run: 13-10-26 · Source: 06\_CAMERA\_V1\_BOOK\_REPAIRED.md · Tool: tools/audit-toc-body.awk (kept, so the audit is reproducible) Command: awk \-v tocend=2659 \-v bodystart=5432 \-f tools/audit-toc-body.awk 06\_CAMERA\_V1\_BOOK\_REPAIRED.md

## 1\. The question, and the answer

The question was whether any scripted chapter's coverage map is built on a wrong entry set, starting from Chapter 51's headline anomaly.  
No scripted chapter's coverage map is built on a wrong entry set. All 27 chapters written so far (Days 1–5) were checked mechanically against the book: every one names its text-less entries, and every claimed subsection total equals the TOC's own count for that chapter. The Chapter 44 error found and fixed earlier the same day was in the LEDGER, not in a chapter file, and it was the only one of its kind.  
But the audit found five things the programme did not know, in §3–§7 below. The most consequential are the cause of the text-less entries — stated in the manuscript itself — and one chapter of prose inside Chapter 56's measured source region.

## 2\. Method, and why it is title-based

The repaired book has three layers, and only two carry course content:

| Layer | Lines | Role |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| CAMERA V1 — GOVERNANCE DOCTRINE | 1–816 | governing principles, not course content |
| MASTER TABLE OF CONTENTS | 817–2659 | the authority for every subsection number and title |
| MASTER CURRICULUM & LESSON SPECIFICATION | 3404–5427 | a separate curriculum document, excluded by the body's start line |
| MASTER COURSE BOOK (the body) | 5432–12,335 | the book: 58 chapters |

Matching cannot be numeric, and this is the trap the audit had to avoid. The body's early chapters carry their subsections as unnumbered headings — the TOC says 1.1 The Purpose of CAMERA and the body says \#\# \*\*The Purpose of CAMERA\*\* — and Chapter 14's subsections are not headings at all (§6). A number-only comparison reports Chapters 1–6 and 14 as having no body text, which is false.  
Three heading styles exist in the body, and the tool matches all three by normalised title:

1. \#\#\# \*\*N.M Title\*\* — Chapters 7–13 and 15–58 (the repair's changes 5 and 6, CHANGES.md PART 2\)  
2. \#\# \*\*Title\*\* — Chapters 1–6 (unnumbered)  
3. \*\*Title\*\* as a whole-line bold paragraph — Chapter 14's twelve subsections

Two further boundaries matter and are parameters, not assumptions: the TOC scan stops at line 2659, before the workbook part of the TOC, whose Workbook 1 / 2 / 3 listings reuse the 1.1, 2.1 … numbering (63 such entries — a number-only sweep of the whole TOC region would silently merge them into course chapters 1, 2, 3); and the body scan starts at 5432, so the lesson-specification layer is never read as body text.

## 3\. Headline numbers

| Measure | Result |
| :---- | :---- |
| TOC entries across Chapters 1–58 | 770 |
| Entries with an exact body heading | 737 |
| Entries with no body heading under that title | 33 |
| — of those, title paraphrases (content present, heading reworded) | 3 |
| — of those, genuine unmatched entries | 30 |
| — of those 30, matching the authoring skip rule (§4) | 28 |
| — of those 30, not explained by the rule | 2 (§5) |
| Body headings no TOC entry covers ("uncited") | 14 |

## 4\. The cause of the unmatched entries is written in the manuscript

The single most useful finding is that the text-less entries are not damage. They are the result of an instruction left in the book itself, at line 11052, inside the misplaced block (§7):  
"Before writing, check the TOC slice I paste below and skip any subsection whose title contains: Exercise, Worksheet, Workshop, Testing, Practical, Practicum, Assignment, or Self-Assessment — those belong in the separate Workbook, not the Book."  
28 of the 30 unmatched entries carry one of those words in their title. So the book is not missing that material by accident: the authoring rule deliberately reserved it for the Workbook. Two independent pieces of evidence in the same manuscript support the reading — the master TOC's workbook part lists the Workbook's own versions of exactly that material (6.1 Hook Bank, 6.3 Retention Checklist, 7.2 Audio, 7.3 Lighting), and the rule names the Workbook as the destination.  
What this changes for the programme. The programme has been discovering these entries a few at a time and ruling on them one at a time — Day 2's four, Chapter 14's two, Day 3's four Practicals, Day 4's four, Day 5's nine. They are 23 of the 30, every one of them in a scripted chapter, and every one is now correctly recorded in its chapter's coverage map. The remaining seven sit in chapters the programme does not script (§5).  
The ledger's own wording on this is too broad and is corrected with this audit. LEDGER.md said "the book-repair audit found five TOC entries with no text anywhere in the book". Those five (§11.12, §11.13, §11.14, §12.15, §31.11) were the ones falling in the chapters then being scripted. The book-wide figure is 30\.

## 5\. The two unmatched entries the rule does not explain

Neither is missing content, and neither is in a chapter the programme scripts. Both were settled by reading the body:

| Entry | Chapter | Finding |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| §3.1 Stop Starting With the Camera | 3 (not scripted) | Present, without a heading. The chapter's opening paragraphs at lines 6227–6231 teach it — "The first mistake most businesses make … is starting with the camera … That reverses the order of the problem." The TOC gives it an entry; the body folds it into the chapter introduction. |
| §54.4 Lighting Check | 54 (not scripted) | Present under a one-word variant. The body heading at line 11981 reads Lighting Setup where the TOC entry at line 2539 reads Lighting Check, and the surrounding sections (§54.1–§54.9) match one for one. Title drift, not a gap. |

So no TOC entry in this book has content that is absent from the book: every one is either deferred to the Workbook by rule (28), folded into an unheaded chapter opening (1), or present under a reworded heading (1).  
The three "paraphrases" are the same phenomenon in Chapter 2, where the body renames the TOC's The Difference Between X and Y as X and Y Are Not the Same Thing (§2.10, §2.11, §2.12). Day 1's Chapter 2 map claims 12 of 12 and all twelve do have body text — the map is right, and a title-matching reader would see three phantom gaps.

## 6\. A repair gap the record did not mention: Chapter 14 — ruled and repaired 13-10-26

CHANGES.md PART 2, change 5, reads "\*\*N.M Title\*\* → \#\#\# \*\*N.M Title\*\* (Ch 14–58) | 527". Chapter 14's twelve subsections were not numbered in the source, so change 5 had nothing to convert there, and change 6 (which numbered Chapters 7–13's plain titles from the TOC) did not cover it either. Measured on the repaired copy: \#\#\# \*\*14.x headings in the body \= 0; whole-line bold subsection titles \= 12\.  
Chapter 14 is therefore the only chapter in the programme whose subsections are not headings, and DAY-3/DAY-3-CH14-prompter.md already noticed and worked around it — its map says the body "marks its twelve subsections as bold titles (not numbered headings)". The audit confirms that and records it as a repair the record does not claim, for John to decide whether Chapter 14 should be normalised to the same standard as the other 51 chapters.  
Ruled 13-10-26: normalise them — and done the same day, as a second repair pass. tools/normalize-ch14-headings.awk (CHANGES.md PART 2B) carries the TOC's twelve Chapter 14 entries as an ordered list and converts a whole-line bold title only when it equals the Nth entry, inside the Chapter 14 region only. The pairing needed no judgement: the twelve body titles and the twelve TOC entries matched 1:1, in order, character for character. Audited line by line against the pre-repair copy: 24 changed lines (12 old, 12 new), 0 blanked, 0 non-title lines altered, the TOC region and every line outside 9106–9220 byte-identical, line count unchanged at 12,335, original sha256 unchanged. Book-wide, whole-line bold titles fall 229 → 217, exactly the twelve converted, and the copy's sha256 is now a94af88aff8d0bece45048e0ca8afad2c3dc4f9a4ebe5ec66e4de77f243297c1.  
The repair changes the basis of one measured figure, and it is recorded rather than left to be discovered. The twelve titles were not heading lines before this pass, so the recorded heading-free source method counted them; now they are excluded like every other heading line. Chapter 14's Day 3 source re-measurement therefore falls 2,138 → 2,116 (22 words, exactly the twelve titles) and the Day 3 region total 16,936 → 16,914. No allowance moves — Day 3's column is retained in full pending its own open ruling — and no scripted chapter's word count, cue span or slide changes. DAY-3/DAY-3-CH14-prompter.md's coverage map, which had noted the bold-title shape and worked around it, now records the normalisation instead.

## 7\. Prose inside a measured source region — Chapter 56

Three sites in the body contain authoring artifacts rather than book prose. Only one sits inside the measured region of a scripted chapter, and it matters because allowances derive from those counts.

| Site | Lines | Words | Whose region |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| editorial line | 8723 | 12 | Chapter 10 (scripted, Day 2\) |
| the misplaced block (§8) | 11049–11079 | 376 | Chapter 39 (not scripted) |
| instructional note | 12116 | 54 | Chapter 56 (scripted, Day 5, not yet written) |

Line 8723 reads "Here's Chapter 11, corrected to end at 11.11 with no exercise/workshop/worksheet content:" — an authoring note, not prose. Line 12116 reads "Keep this section consistent with the Chapter Contract's standing rule that AI is a workflow tool …" — the same kind of note.  
The consequence, measured, and deliberately not applied. Chapter 56's source is tabled at 2,037 words; 54 of them are the instruction at line 12116\. Subtracting them gives 1,983 and moves the Day's source total 16,919 → 16,865 — and because the Day 5 formula is 10,500 × chapter source ÷ Day source, a correction to the denominator moves every allowance, not just this one. Both coherent routes were computed rather than guessed:

| Route | What it does | Result | Problem |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| A — correct Chapter 56 only | allowance from 10,500 × 1,983 ÷ 16,919 as tabled | Chapter 56 1,265 → 1,231 | the nine allowances then sum to 10,466, breaking the convention that the column equals 10,500 exactly |
| B — recompute the column | all nine from 10,500 × source ÷ 16,865, 2 words given back on the last chapter | 1,353 / 699 / 1,574 / 1,009 / 1,017 / 1,207 / 1,021 / 1,387 / 1,233 \= 10,500 | it re-opens three already-written chapters — Chapter 31 1,349→1,353, Chapter 33 696→699, Chapter 43 1,569→1,574 — the budget they were measured against |
| C — no budget change | keep the column; record that Chapter 56's measured source includes 54 words of artifact | no figure moves | Chapter 56's allowance stays marginally overstated |

My recommendation is C, with the defect recorded, unless John prefers B. The Day 4 precedent governs: when that Day's source total was corrected from 17,227 to 17,196 the six recomputed allowances were disclosed beside the issued ones rather than substituted, because the issued figures are the budget the chapters were written to. Route B is defensible precisely because Chapter 56 is not yet written, so nothing is re-opened mid-flight — but it also moves three chapters that are finished, and that trade is John's to make. Nothing has been changed by this audit. Chapter 10's 12-word contamination sits inside a Day that is complete and whose allowances are tabled; it is recorded for the same reason and changes no figure by itself.  
Ruled 13-10-26: John took route A. Chapter 56's source is corrected to 1,983, its allowance shrunk alone to 1,231 (from 1,265, ceiling 1,267, 9:51), and the Day's column left at 10,466 — 34 words, 16 seconds of the Day's 84:00, budgeted to no chapter — where it had summed to 10,500; ceilings read 10,776 and planned minutes 83:43. The three written chapters route B would have moved — Chapters 31, 33 and 43 — are untouched, which is what route A buys. Chapter 10's 12 words were ruled the same day to be recorded and not subtracted, for the reason given here: its Day is complete and no figure it was judged against should move. The applied arithmetic, and the four forward projections in LEDGER.md that depended on the old Chapter 56 allowance, are corrected at their own rows there.

## 8\. Chapter 51's anomaly, resolved and renamed

The ledger carried "§51.7 Backup and Archive is duplicated into the wrong chapter (anomaly F, CHANGES.md)". CHANGES.md contains no anomaly letters, and what it documents in PART 3 is wider and more useful than that sentence: the misplaced block at lines 11056–11079, which John instructed be reported and left alone. Measured on the repaired copy, the block contains:

* 11056 — a pasted \#\# \*\*Chapter 51 — Software Workflows\*\* heading  
* 11057–11070 — a pasted TOC slice of Chapter 51's seven subsection headings, §51.1–§51.7  
* 11071–11079 — an empty heading at 11072, the authoring note at 11049–11052 and 11074, and a duplicate of §50.10

So it is not a duplicate of Chapter 51's content. It is a slice of Chapter 51's index — seven headings with no prose under them — pasted into Chapter 39's region during authoring. Chapter 51's real body is intact at 11814 onward, 7 of 7, which the audit confirms independently: Chapter 51 is one of the six clean TOC-to-body matches in Day 5, and the seven headings the audit reports as misfiled in Chapter 39 are exactly this slice. The ledger's description is corrected to say so, and the audit's "uncited: 7" for Chapter 39 is this block and nothing else.

## 9\. What was checked and found clean

* 27 scripted chapters: every text-less entry is named in its coverage map; every claimed total equals the TOC count; no map presents a gap as covered.  
* Chapter 51's mandated set of 7 — all seven headings present in its own body. The block in Chapter 39 does not touch it.  
* Every other chapter's entry set — 737 of 770 TOC entries match exactly, including all 19 of Chapter 29's, all 15 of Chapter 21's and all 14 of Chapter 43's — the three clean chapters of Days 4 and 5\.  
* Uncited body headings, the reverse direction, number 14 in the whole book: 3 in Chapter 2 and 3 in Chapter 6 (inert worksheet frames), the 7 of the misplaced block, and 1 in Chapter 54 (§5). None is taught course content that a map should have carried.

## 10\. Open items this audit puts to John — all settled 13-10-26

1. Chapter 56's source and allowance — ruled: route A, shrink it alone. The 54 words at line 12116 are removed, so its source is 1,983 and the Day total 16,865; the allowance is recomputed from the corrected source against the as-tabled denominator of 16,919 so that no written chapter's budget moves — 1,231 (from 1,265), ceiling 1,267, 9:51. This item's own re-verification is the finding: the nine allowances now sum to 10,466, not 10,500. That is route A's known cost — 34 words (16 seconds) of the Day budgeted to no chapter — accepted because route B, which would have restored 10,500, was rejected: it moved Chapter 31 (1,349→1,353), Chapter 33 (696→699) and Chapter 43 (1,569→1,574), already written and judged against the issued figures. Applied to LEDGER.md's Day 5 table and measuring notes, and to the four forward projections that depended on the old figure.  
2. Chapter 14's twelve subsections — ruled: normalise them. Done the same day as a second repair pass (CHANGES.md PART 2B, §6 above): 12 lines, verified line by line, copy sha256 now a94af88a…, book at 58 of 58 chapters and 1,647 numbered subsection headings. Chapter 14's heading-free Day 3 source moves 2,138 → 2,116 as a consequence (its twelve titles are heading lines now) and the Day 3 region 16,936 → 16,914; no allowance moves.  
3. Chapter 10's 12 words — ruled: record, do not subtract. Its Day is complete and its allowances are the figures its nine chapters were written and judged against, so the treatment licensed for the unwritten Chapter 56 is unavailable there.  
4. The misplaced block — ruled: leave it in the copy, unrepaired and undeleted. It sits in Chapter 39, which the programme does not script, so it measures nothing and moves no figure; §8 above is now its record, and CHANGES.md PART 3 carries the ruling.  
5. The ledger's own two overstatements — corrected in place with this audit, as reported here: the "five entries … anywhere in the book" scope, and the anomaly-F description.

# DAY 5 — DAY REPORT

Day: 5 of 5 · Chapters: 31, 33, 43, 44, 47, 48, 50, 51, 56 · Scripts: CAMERA\_V2/DAY-5/, all nine written to budget and each carrying its own Chapter Report Reported: 13-10-26, after Chapter 56 — the last chapter of the Day and of the programme's live teaching · Prepared by: Freebuff Format: the Section 5 Day Report: total words against 10,500, total speech minutes, total interaction minutes, grand total against 120, a rebuilt run sheet with measured times for every block, and the PRE-WORK percentage.  
Status: PASS on the spoken script and on the live ceiling, with the second-widest live clearance of the five Days. 10,453 spoken words against the 10,500 limit (47 words, 23 seconds under) and 119:37 live against the 120:00 ceiling (23 seconds of clearance), with interaction at the mandated 36:00. Every chapter passes its own five checks. The Day's nine chapters are 1,348 / 689 / 1,569 / 1,000 / 1,013 / 1,203 / 1,018 / 1,382 / 1,231 — six exactly on their allowance and three under it, and not one over its own for the first time in the programme.  
Day 5 is the first Day in which six chapters land exactly on their allowance, and the first whose break sits on a chapter cue. Day 3 is the programme's other Day with no chapter over its allowance; what is new here is the exactness — six of the nine chapters land on the word, and the three that do not are deliberately under rather than loosely under. And the item Days 3 and 4 both reported — a mandated break that no cue places — has a home in Day 5's scripts: Chapter 33's Slide 11 cue reads "This is the Day's break point — the one cue in Day 5 that does host it", at the end of the Instructor Review. Days 1 and 2 place their breaks in their Day Reports and no chapter cue; Day 5 is the first Day whose placement is carried by the script itself. The placement is still a proposal rather than a ruling, and Section 8.1 sets out what it changes.  
One item in this Day has no precedent in the programme and is put to John in Section 8.3. The book's Chapter 56 is written on the assumption that Chapters 52 through 55 have run — "a setup, five scripts, five recordings, five published videos" — and those four chapters are not in this programme at all. The chapter is scripted with every subsection's core claim intact and its material grounded in the chapters the programme does deliver, and no slide claims the cycle was run; the substitution is disclosed in the file, in the ledger and in Section 8.3.

Why it fits, and how the margin compares. Day 3's clearance remains the widest in the programme — 412 words and 3:18 live, because its chapters were written well under their allowances — and Day 5 is the second-widest on the live clock with 23 seconds inside, against Day 4's 11 and Day 2's 9\. John's ruling of 13-10-26 shrank Chapter 56 alone, which left the nine allowances summing to 10,466 rather than 10,500 — 34 words of the Day budgeted to no chapter. The Day then spent 10,453, so it came in 13 words under its own budget and 47 under Section 2's limit, and the clearance exists for a reason no earlier Day had: six of the nine chapters landed exactly on their allowance rather than drifting inside their tolerance. Chapter 31's −1, Chapter 33's −7 and Chapter 44's −5 are the whole of the Day's underspend.  
The one arithmetic convention. Chapter minutes are each rounded from their own word count: the nine delivered figures sum to 5,017 seconds \= 83:37, and the Day's 10,453 words divided by 125 gives 5,017.44 seconds \= 83:37 — the same figure from both directions, so there is no one-second gap to explain. Every block on the run sheet below is a chapter or a mandated interaction segment.

The ceiling column is the Day 1 trap, shown a fifth time. If all nine chapters had landed at the top of their \+3% tolerance the Day would read 10,776 words \= 86:12, and adding the 36 minutes of interaction would put it at 122:12 — 2:12 over the ceiling. Day 5's tolerance column is not the programme's worst: Day 4's reaches 10,812 words \= 86:30 and 122:30 live, and Day 1's 10,813-word column reaches the same 122:30. What is true of all five Days is that the tolerances are individually legitimate and collectively unaffordable, and what is true of Day 5 is that it used almost none of them — which is the whole of why both of its limits are met with room.  
The Day is also the first in which six of nine chapters landed exactly on their allowance, and that is a drafting result rather than a coincidence. Chapters 43, 47, 48, 50, 51 and 56 each landed on the word, in five different sessions, and the three that did not were each deliberately under: Chapter 31 by one word, Chapter 33 by seven and Chapter 44 by five. Nothing was written to tolerance and nothing was left long.

Unlike Day 3, the two bases agree here, so nothing about this Day's presentation is ambiguous — and the Day 5 formula's own target of 1.68 : 1 was not reached because three of the nine chapters — the software stack, the workflows and the capstone — are long-source, low-allowance builder material.

| Total interaction | 36:00 | exactly the mandated minimum |

Day 5 concentrates its whole live block in one chapter, and it is the first Day to do so. Chapters 31, 43, 44, 47, 48, 50, 51 and 56 are pure teaching — every second of their spans is speech — while Chapter 33 carries all four of the Day's exercises at five minutes each: §33.7 Recording Exercise, §33.8 Editing Exercise, §33.9 Peer Review and §33.10 Instructor Review. That is the Day's entire 20-minute exercise budget in one workshop block, where Days 3 and 4 spread theirs across chapters. The split is proposed rather than settled, and it is the fourth successive Day to carry an unsettled exercise decision.  
No homework is set by Day 5\. Eight of the nine Chapter Reports state it explicitly and Chapter 56 states why: the fifteen builder sections produce the Business Content System document, and that document is written by the attendee as the programme's own capstone assignment — Section 9's Chapter 32 brief and nineteen-step checklist — rather than as homework set by a chapter. The Day therefore sets no evening work, and the close names the assignment instead.

## 5\. PRE-WORK

0 of 16,865 source words, 0%. All 100 mandated subsections are covered — 10 \+ 10 \+ 14 \+ 13 \+ 9 \+ 13 \+ 10 \+ 7 \+ 15, from 101 TOC entries — and no subsection is marked PRE-WORK, so Day 5 adds nothing to the Pre-Work Pack and uses none of the 20% cap (3,373 words available).  
The Day's coverage composition, in full. §31.11 AI Tool Decision Worksheet is dropped on John's recorded ruling and is the Day's only struck-through entry; the four-question filter it would have carried is taught in full at §31.10 and carried as Chapter 31's supplementary board. §33.7–§33.10 are authored as Day 5's four live exercises at five minutes each. §44.9 Hook Testing, §44.10 Format Testing, §44.11 Topic Testing and §44.12 CTA Testing are authored as spoken teaching on John's ruling of 13-10-26, written in the improvement loop's own vocabulary. Every other subsection in the Day is spoken from the book's body, and six of the nine chapters — 43, 47, 48, 50, 51 and 56 — are clean TOC-to-body matches that depend on no ruling at all.

No FAIL on the words or the live clock, and no arithmetic decision goes to John. Day 5 meets both hard limits with all nine chapters passing their own five checks and every chapter at or under its allowance. The two items in Section 8 are a proposed placement and a disclosed substitution; the outstanding work for Day 5 is approval, not correction.

## 7\. What this Day proves about the budget method

| Day | Words | vs 10,500 | Live | vs 120:00 | Chapters over their allowance | Result |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | 10,633 | \+133 (1:04 over) | 121:04 | 1:04 over | 4 of 5 | FAIL, accepted as an exception |
| 2 | 10,484 | −16 (8 seconds under) | 119:51 | 9 seconds inside | 3 of 7 | PASS |
| 3 | 10,088 | −412 (3:18 under) | 116:42 | 3:18 inside | 0 of 5 | PASS |
| 4 | 10,478 | −22 (11 seconds under) | 119:49 | 11 seconds inside | 1 of 6 | PASS |
| 5 | 10,453 | −47 (23 seconds under) | 119:37 | 23 seconds inside | 0 of 9 | PASS |

The "chapters over" column now reads 4, 3, 0, 1, 0 and it is the whole story of the Day 1 breach and everything that followed. Day 1 cut each chapter to its own allowance and accepted all four overs, which summed to a breach. Day 2 wrote its last four deliberately under theirs to net −16. Day 3 spent no tolerance at all. Day 4 spent 13 words of it on one chapter and still landed under, because that chapter was written to the words the Day had left rather than to the ceiling it was entitled to. Day 5 spent none: nine chapters, six of them landing on the exact word and three deliberately under, for the second-widest live clearance of the five Days.  
Two things Day 5 shows that earlier Days did not. The first is that exactness is achievable at scale — six chapters landing on their allowance to the word was not planned as a target and is not a rounding artefact: each was cut or restored to the number, which took one pass plus a restoration on Chapters 50, 51 and 56, two passes on Chapters 43 and 47, and four counting rounds on Chapter 48\. The second is that a Day's budget can be smaller than the sum of its allowances and still close cleanly: John's ruling left 34 words of the Day unassigned, and the Day finished 13 words under what remained. Neither would have been possible with a chapter written to tolerance.

## 8\. The open items, ranked

### 8.1 The mid-session break — carried by a cue for the first time, and the placement is still a proposal

The facts, and how Day 5 differs from Days 3 and 4\. Section 5 says every Day uses the same skeleton: opening 6, break 5, close 5, exercises 20, total interaction 36\. Days 3 and 4 both reported that no cue in their files placed the break, and Day 4's report recorded the item as programme-level. Day 5 places it: because Chapter 33 holds the Day's entire live block, its Instructor Review ends the interactive half of the Day, and Chapter 33's Slide 11 cue names that point as the break — the first Day 5 cue to host it, and the only Day in the programme so far whose break sits on a cue rather than in a report.  
The two readings, and they still matter here.

| Reading | Interaction | Live | Against 120:00 | Against the 36:00 minimum |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| A — the break stands where the cue names it (after Chapter 33's Instructor Review; the report's reading) | 36:00 | 119:37 | PASS, 23 seconds clear | PASS |
| B — the break is dropped (opening 6 \+ close 5 \+ exercises 20\) | 31:00 | 114:37 | PASS | FAIL — 5:00 short |

My recommendation is Reading A, and Day 5 is the cheapest place to settle it — the cue already exists, so ratifying the placement costs no edit to any file, while Days 3 and 4 would each need one hand-off cue reworded. The item is unchanged in substance: two readings exist, B fails the interaction minimum by five minutes, and only John can rule which applies. What Day 5 adds is that the programme now has a working precedent for where the break belongs — at the end of the block that carries the Day's exercises — and that the precedent is carried by a cue rather than by a report, which is the difference between a placement the presenter sees during the Day and one only a reader of the ledger sees. Days 1 and 2 each place their break in their Day Reports (Day 1 in its interaction accounting, Day 2 after Chapter 9\) and in no chapter cue; Days 3 and 4 have neither. Day 5 is the first Day where the script itself carries it.

### 8.2 Day 5's exercise split — proposed, not settled

All four exercises at five minutes each sit in Chapter 33 — §33.7 Recording Exercise, §33.8 Editing Exercise, §33.9 Peer Review, §33.10 Instructor Review — 20 minutes, the whole live-exercise allowance, in one chapter. This replaces the Day 1 default 6 \+ 8 \+ 6, which cannot apply here because Day 5's four exercise headings are its own and the chapters they were written for are not in this Day. It is proposed and flagged rather than settled, and it is the fourth successive Day with an unsettled exercise decision.  
Two things follow from concentrating the whole block in one chapter, and both are disclosed rather than discovered later. The eight other chapters are pure teaching, so any re-cut of Chapter 33 that moved its speech would move the Day's only interactive block with it; and the Day's interaction depends on that single block plus the mandated segments, so there is no slack elsewhere in the schedule if the exercises run long. A 6 \+ 8 \+ 6 or 5 \+ 5 \+ 5 \+ 5 split elsewhere would need an exercise heading to move to a chapter that does not carry one, which is why the proposal is what it is.

### 8.3 Chapter 56's source assumes four chapters the programme does not run

This item has no precedent in the programme and it is a scripted chapter, not a plan. The book's Chapter 56 opens by referring to "Chapters 52 through 55 — a setup, five scripts, five recordings, five published videos"; §56.9 asks for "the actual setup you built and tested in Chapter 52"; and §56.11 asks the workflow to be grounded in "what you actually practiced across Chapters 52 through 55". Chapters 52 through 55 are not in this programme: Day 5's set is 31, 33, 43, 44, 47, 48, 50, 51 and 56, no Day's table lists 52–55, and Section 9 gives Chapter 32, not Chapter 52, as the capstone assignment.  
What was done, and why it was the only honest option. Section 3's promise rule forbids a script from promising something the programme does not deliver, so no slide in Chapter 56 claims the cycle was run. Every subsection keeps its core claim and its book citation, and the material is grounded in what the programme does deliver — the gear framework of Chapters 43, 47 and 48 at §56.9, Chapter 51's routes and this Day's own recording and editing from Chapter 33's workshop block at §56.11, and Chapter 51's Content Calendar → Production route at §56.12. Nothing is dropped, nothing becomes PRE-WORK, and no word count moves.  
What John is being asked to confirm, and the alternatives. The alternatives were to script the chapter as though the cycle had run — which would be a false promise — or to mark the three affected subsections PRE-WORK, which would drop three of the capstone's fifteen sections out of the Day. The treatment applied is the third option and the only one that keeps the Day's coverage at 100 of 100 while claiming nothing untrue, and it is put to John because the substitution should be his to accept rather than the executor's to assume. If he rules otherwise, the three subsections are the only ones affected, and the chapter's figures would not change.

### 8.4 Day 2's exercise split — still unconfirmed

Day 2's 6 \+ 8 \+ 6 across Chapters 7, 8 and 9 remains unconfirmed, carried forward from Day 4's report so it is not lost. Days 3 and 4's splits were released with their Days' approvals; Days 2 and 5 are the two still open, and Day 5's is now the larger of the two because it holds the whole Day's interaction.

## 9\. Limits on what this report claims

* No visuals exist for Day 5\. All nine files carry their Phase D visual specifications — 12 / 12 / 16 / 15 / 11 / 15 / 12 / 9 / 17 entries, one per slide plus a supplementary, build-step totals 31 / 30 / 43 / 37 / 30 / 41 / 33 / 26 / 44 \= 315 — and nothing is built: every deck is still stopped at the Step A look gate, so no deck, animation or on-screen timing can be checked. The only built artifacts in the programme remain the Chapter 1 style lock and the Chapter 10 density prototype.  
* No timing evidence. 125 wpm is the working rate from Section 4\. Every minute figure in this report is arithmetic from a program word count, not a stopwatch reading, and Day 5's 23-second clearance is inside the tolerance of that projection rather than proof of it. The arithmetic of the clearance, stated plainly: the Day's live total is 119:37 against a 120:00 ceiling, and its speech is 83:37 against the 84:00 speech ceiling, so any pace slower than the working rate by about half a percent — 47 words' worth across the whole Day — puts Day 5 over both limits at once. The clearance is wider than any earlier Day's and it is still a projection's clearance, not a measurement of delivery.  
* Two source measures disagree, as they have for every Day, and Day 5's gap is the widest at 4.3%. Section 4 fixes Day 5's source at 17,672; the recorded heading-free count on the repaired copy gives 16,865 — the figure the nine allowances were derived from and the one this report uses, with the 54-word authoring note inside Chapter 56 removed on John's ruling. The nine allowances are therefore retained as issued — 1,349 / 696 / 1,569 / 1,005 / 1,013 / 1,203 / 1,018 / 1,382 / 1,231 — summing to 10,466 rather than 10,500, with 34 words (16 seconds) of the Day budgeted to no chapter. The Day's 84:00 speech ceiling is untouched by that, and the Day closes 23 seconds of speech inside it — 83:37 against 84:00.  
* The 36:00 interaction figure depends on the Section 8.1 ruling. It is the reading in which Chapter 33's cue places the break. Reading B gives 31:00 and a Day FAIL; neither this report nor the ledger should be read as having settled which applies until John rules.  
* The four authored entries in Chapter 33 and the four in Chapter 44 are decisions, not measurements. Every count above is a program count; the judgement that §33.7–§33.10 are authored as the Day's live exercises, and that §44.9–§44.12 are authored as spoken teaching, comes from John's rulings of 13-10-26 and from the proposal tabled in the ledger, and is restated here so neither can later be mistaken for the book having carried text for them.  
* Chapter 56's substituted provenance is disclosed in Section 8.3 and is not a settled treatment. The chapter's coverage, word count and spans are measured and final; the judgement that its material may be grounded in this Day's chapters rather than in the Chapters 52–55 cycle is put to John.  
* Day 5's chapters await approval. Only Chapter 1 is approved across the programme. All nine Day 5 chapters are written, measured and reported, and each is gated on John's "approved" before any visual work may begin.  
* Local files only. Everything above is measured on the nine files in CAMERA\_V2/DAY-5/; nothing has been deployed, published or sent anywhere.
